I don’t know if that counts, but it’s definitely not the same as it used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or sad most of the day, more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say most of the day, exactly. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days, you feel down or sad for the majority of the day, even if you're still able to get things done?\n\nPatient: I… I’m honestly not sure. I’m not totally sure. I think there have been periods like that, where I’ll feel low for part of the day, but I don’t know if I’d say it was most days over two years.

My appetite goes up and down, and there are stretches where I’ll skip dinner without really meaning to, and I’ve lost some weight without trying. Other nights I’ll just pick at crackers or cereal because cooking for just myself feels… I don’t know, kind of pointless. It’s probably not a huge thing, but it’s been going on for months, so I thought I should maybe talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t enjoy things as much as you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you once liked?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I mean, I still try to do the things I used to enjoy, like watering the garden or going to book club, so it’s not like I’ve stopped completely. But… it often feels kind of flat. I’ll show up and then leave early, or I’ll think about starting something and just not. I don’t know if that counts, but it’s definitely not the same as it used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or sad most of the day, more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say most of the day, exactly. I mean, I have moments where I feel kind of low or… heavy, I guess, but I can still get through what I need to do. Maybe I’ve been more subdued than usual. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days, you feel down or sad for the majority of the day, even if you're still able to get things done?\n\nPatient: I… I’m honestly not sure. I don’t want to overstate it. There are definitely days where I feel low for a good chunk of the day, but “most days” and “majority of the day” sounds so definite. I still manage to function, so I guess I hesitate to say yes. Maybe it’s more like it comes and goes, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or lacking in energy a lot of the time, even if you’re not doing much?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m still doing my job and getting through the day. But even when my caseload isn’t especially heavy, I feel like I’m sort of dragging myself through the hospital.\n\nAnd then when I get home, I’m just… done. Too tired to cook properly, or stretch, or even answer personal messages sometimes. I know that probably doesn’t sound huge, but it’s been noticeable.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or blaming yourself for things more than usual, or feeling like you're not worth much?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I don’t want to make too much of it, but I do catch myself replaying my marriage and thinking, you know, I must have failed at something pretty basic. Like I should have been better at it somehow.\n\nPart of me knows relationships are more complicated than one person “failing,” but… I still go there in my head. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or sad on most days, even if it's not all day long?\n\nPatient: I’m… still hesitant to say “most days,” honestly. Some days, yes, I do feel kind of low or flat, but then other days I’m more okay, or at least distracted enough that I don’t really notice it as sadness.\n\nSo I don’t know if I should call it most days. I don’t want to overstate it. Maybe it’s more like it’s been there off and on, and I’m not always sure how to measure that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that over the past two weeks, you’ve had more days than not where you felt down or sad, even if it wasn’t all day?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I think… maybe not more days than not, if I’m being careful with the wording. There have definitely been days where I felt down, but there are also days where I’m functioning and I don’t notice it as much.\n\nI don’t want to answer in a way that makes it sound worse than it is. So I’d probably say it’s been intermittent, not every other day necessarily… unless I’m undercounting it. I don’t want to minimize it if it matters, but I also don’t want to make it sound constant when I’m not sure it has been.\n\nDoctor: Over the past few months, would you say that on most days you’ve had some period of time—maybe not the whole day, but a significant part of the day—where you felt down, sad, or low?\n\nPatient: Over the past few months… I’m honestly not sure I can say “most days.” There are definitely times during the day where I feel low or sort of heavy, but I don’t know if it’s been a significant part of the day on most days.\n\nI might be kind of splitting hairs, I know, but I don’t want to make it sound more constant than it is. Other nights I’ll just pick at crackers or cereal because cooking for just myself feels… I don’t know, kind of pointless. It’s probably not a huge thing, but it’s been going on for months, so I thought I should maybe talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t enjoy things as much as you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you once liked?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I mean, I still try to do the things I used to enjoy, like watering the garden or going to book club, so it’s not like I’ve stopped completely. But… it often feels kind of flat. I’ll show up and then leave early, or I’ll think about starting something and just not. I don’t know if that counts, but it’s definitely not the same as it used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or sad most of the day, more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say most of the day, exactly. I mean, I have moments where I feel kind of low or… heavy, I guess, but I can still get through what I need to do. Maybe I’ve been more subdued than usual. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days, you feel down or sad for the majority of the day, even if you're still able to get things done?\n\nPatient: I… I’m honestly not sure. I don’t want to overstate it. There are definitely days where I feel low for a good chunk of the day, but “most days” and “majority of the day” sounds so definite. I still manage to function, so I guess I hesitate to say yes. Maybe it’s more like it comes and goes, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or lacking in energy a lot of the time, even if you’re not doing much?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m still doing my job and getting through the day. But even when my caseload isn’t especially heavy, I feel like I’m sort of dragging myself through the hospital.\n\nAnd then when I get home, I’m just… done. Too tired to cook properly, or stretch, or even answer personal messages sometimes. I know that probably doesn’t sound huge, but it’s been noticeable.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or blaming yourself for things more than usual, or feeling like you're not worth much?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I don’t want to make too much of it, but I do catch myself replaying my marriage and thinking, you know, I must have failed at something pretty basic. Like I should have been better at it somehow.\n\nPart of me knows relationships are more complicated than one person “failing,” but… I still go there in my head. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or sad on most days, even if it's not all day long?\n\nPatient: I’m… still hesitant to say “most days,” honestly. Some days, yes, I do feel kind of low or flat, but then other days I’m more okay, or at least distracted enough that I don’t really notice it as sadness.\n\nSo I don’t know if I should call it most days. I don’t want to overstate it. Maybe it’s more like it’s been there off and on, and I’m not always sure how to measure that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that over the past two weeks, you’ve had more days than not where you felt down or sad, even if it wasn’t all day?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I think… maybe not more days than not, if I’m being careful with the wording. Other nights I’ll just pick at crackers or cereal because cooking for just myself feels… I don’t know, kind of pointless. It’s probably not a huge thing, but it’s been going on for months, so I thought I should maybe talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t enjoy things as much as you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you once liked?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I mean, I still try to do the things I used to enjoy, like watering the garden or going to book club, so it’s not like I’ve stopped completely. But… it often feels kind of flat. I’ll show up and then leave early, or I’ll think about starting something and just not. I don’t know if that counts, but it’s definitely not the same as it used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or sad most of the day, more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say most of the day, exactly. I mean, I have moments where I feel kind of low or… heavy, I guess, but I can still get through what I need to do. Maybe I’ve been more subdued than usual. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days, you feel down or sad for the majority of the day, even if you're still able to get things done?\n\nPatient: I… I’m honestly not sure. I don’t want to overstate it. There are definitely days where I feel low for a good chunk of the day, but “most days” and “majority of the day” sounds so definite. I still manage to function, so I guess I hesitate to say yes. Maybe it’s more like it comes and goes, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or lacking in energy a lot of the time, even if you’re not doing much?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m still doing my job and getting through the day. But even when my caseload isn’t especially heavy, I feel like I’m sort of dragging myself through the hospital.\n\nAnd then when I get home, I’m just… done. Too tired to cook properly, or stretch, or even answer personal messages sometimes. I know that probably doesn’t sound huge, but it’s been noticeable.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or blaming yourself for things more than usual, or feeling like you're not worth much?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I don’t want to make too much of it, but I do catch myself replaying my marriage and thinking, you know, I must have failed at something pretty basic. Like I should have been better at it somehow.\n\nPart of me knows relationships are more complicated than one person “failing,” but… I still go there in my head. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or sad on most days, even if it's not all day long?\n\nPatient: I’m… still hesitant to say “most days,” honestly. Some days, yes, I do feel kind of low or flat, but then other days I’m more okay, or at least distracted enough that I don’t really notice it as sadness.\n\nSo I don’t know if I should call it most days. I don’t want to overstate it. Maybe it’s more like it’s been there off and on, and I’m not always sure how to measure that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that over the past two weeks, you’ve had more days than not where you felt down or sad, even if it wasn’t all day?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I think… maybe not more days than not, if I’m being careful with the wording. I mean, there have been stretches where I’ve felt off and kind of sad, but when you say “two weeks straight,” I’m not sure I can honestly say that.\n\nIt’s more like it comes and goes. I don’t want to minimize it if it matters, but I also don’t want to make it sound constant when I’m not sure it has been.\n\nDoctor: Over the past few months, would you say that on most days you’ve had some period of time—maybe not the whole day, but a significant part of the day—where you felt down, sad, or low?\n\nPatient: Over the past few months… I’m honestly not sure I can say “most days.” There are definitely times during the day where I feel low or sort of heavy, but I don’t know if it’s been a significant part of the day on most days.\n\nI might be kind of splitting hairs, I know, but I don’t want to make it sound more constant than it is. Maybe it’s been there more than I’ve been admitting to myself, but I’m not fully sure how to answer that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you had days where you felt down or sad for most of the day, not just off and on?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s been most of the day, no. There have been days where I feel sad or kind of low, but it tends to come in waves, or I notice it more at certain points.\n\nI’m trying to answer that accurately… I don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely fine, but “most of the day” feels stronger than what I’ve noticed. Does that seem like the right way to describe it?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you take less pleasure or interest in things you used to enjoy, even when you still do them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably true, though I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nI still do some of the things I normally like—like I’ll water the garden, or I’ll go to book club—but a lot of the time it just feels kind of flat. Like I’m doing the right things, but I’m not really getting much from them. Sometimes I leave early, or I don’t even get started, which isn’t really like me.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, would you say that you've had more days than not where you felt down or sad most of the day, even if you were still able to get things done?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that, no… at least not clearly.\n\nOver two years is hard for me to judge. I’ve definitely had stretches where I felt low, but “more days than not” and “most of the day” sounds like more than I can confidently say. I’ve mostly kept functioning, so I may not have been paying close attention to it.\n\nMaybe I’m minimizing it a little, but I don’t think I can honestly say it’s been that persistent. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, would you say that on most days you had some period of time — maybe not the whole day, but a noticeable part of it — where you felt down, sad, or low?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I think there have been periods like that, where I’ll feel low for part of the day, but I don’t know if I’d say it was most days over two years.\n\nIt’s hard to separate normal stress or tiredness from feeling down, if that makes sense. I don’t want to minimize it, but I also don’t want to make it sound more constant than it’s been. I guess I’ve noticed it sometimes, just not in a way I can confidently say was happening most days. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you had times when you felt down or low almost every day, even if it wasn’t all day, for a period of at least two years?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s been a full two years, no.\n\nIf I’m being more precise, it really seems like it started after my divorce was finalized… which was about a year and seven months ago. Since then there have been periods where I’ve felt down very regularly, but I don’t want to say “at least two years” if that isn’t quite accurate.\n\nIs that distinction helpful?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you had days where you felt down or sad nearly every day for periods lasting at least two years?\n\nPatient: Right… I think I might have said this a bit unclearly before. I don’t think it’s been a full two years.\n\nIt’s really been since my divorce was finalized, which was about a year and seven months ago. Since then, yes, I’ve had a lot of days where I’ve felt down or sad, but I don’t want to overstate it and say it lasted at least two years when it hasn’t quite been that long.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I still try to do the things I used to enjoy, like watering the garden or going to book club, so it’s not like I’ve stopped completely. But… it often feels kind of flat. I’ll show up and then leave early, or I’ll think about starting something and just not. I don’t know if that counts, but it’s definitely not the same as it used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or sad most of the day, more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say most of the day, exactly. I mean, I have moments where I feel kind of low or… heavy, I guess, but I can still get through what I need to do. Maybe I’ve been more subdued than usual. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days, you feel down or sad for the majority of the day, even if you're still able to get things done?\n\nPatient: I… I’m honestly not sure. I don’t want to overstate it. There are definitely days where I feel low for a good chunk of the day, but “most days” and “majority of the day” sounds so definite. I still manage to function, so I guess I hesitate to say yes. Maybe it’s more like it comes and goes, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or lacking in energy a lot of the time, even if you’re not doing much?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m still doing my job and getting through the day. But even when my caseload isn’t especially heavy, I feel like I’m sort of dragging myself through the hospital.\n\nAnd then when I get home, I’m just… done. Too tired to cook properly, or stretch, or even answer personal messages sometimes. I know that probably doesn’t sound huge, but it’s been noticeable.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or blaming yourself for things more than usual, or feeling like you're not worth much?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I don’t want to make too much of it, but I do catch myself replaying my marriage and thinking, you know, I must have failed at something pretty basic. Like I should have been better at it somehow.\n\nPart of me knows relationships are more complicated than one person “failing,” but… I still go there in my head. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or sad on most days, even if it's not all day long?\n\nPatient: I’m… still hesitant to say “most days,” honestly. Some days, yes, I do feel kind of low or flat, but then other days I’m more okay, or at least distracted enough that I don’t really notice it as sadness.\n\nSo I don’t know if I should call it most days. I don’t want to overstate it. Maybe it’s more like it’s been there off and on, and I’m not always sure how to measure that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that over the past two weeks, you’ve had more days than not where you felt down or sad, even if it wasn’t all day?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I think… maybe not more days than not, if I’m being careful with the wording. There have definitely been days where I felt down, but there are also days where I’m functioning and I don’t notice it as much.\n\nI don’t want to answer in a way that makes it sound worse than it is. So I’d probably say it’s been intermittent, not every other day necessarily… unless I’m undercounting it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Over the past few weeks, have you had any periods where you felt down or sad for two weeks straight, even if it wasn't every single day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not two weeks straight. I mean, there have been stretches where I’ve felt off and kind of sad, but when you say “two weeks straight,” I’m not sure I can honestly say that.\n\nIt’s more like it comes and goes. I don’t want to minimize it if it matters, but I also don’t want to make it sound constant when I’m not sure it has been.\n\nDoctor: Over the past few months, would you say that on most days you’ve had some period of time—maybe not the whole day, but a significant part of the day—where you felt down, sad, or low?\n\nPatient: Over the past few months… I’m honestly not sure I can say “most days.” There are definitely times during the day where I feel low or sort of heavy, but I don’t know if it’s been a significant part of the day on most days.\n\nI might be kind of splitting hairs, I know, but I don’t want to make it sound more constant than it is. Maybe it’s been there more than I’ve been admitting to myself, but I’m not fully sure how to answer that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you had days where you felt down or sad for most of the day, not just off and on?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s been most of the day, no. There have been days where I feel sad or kind of low, but it tends to come in waves, or I notice it more at certain points.\n\nI’m trying to answer that accurately… I don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely fine, but “most of the day” feels stronger than what I’ve noticed. Does that seem like the right way to describe it?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you take less pleasure or interest in things you used to enjoy, even when you still do them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably true, though I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nI still do some of the things I normally like—like I’ll water the garden, or I’ll go to book club—but a lot of the time it just feels kind of flat. Like I’m doing the right things, but I’m not really getting much from them. Sometimes I leave early, or I don’t even get started, which isn’t really like me.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, would you say that you've had more days than not where you felt down or sad most of the day, even if you were still able to get things done?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that, no… at least not clearly.\n\nOver two years is hard for me to judge. I’ve definitely had stretches where I felt low, but “more days than not” and “most of the day” sounds like more than I can confidently say. I’ve mostly kept functioning, so I may not have been paying close attention to it.\n\nMaybe I’m minimizing it a little, but I don’t think I can honestly say it’s been that persistent. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, would you say that on most days you had some period of time — maybe not the whole day, but a noticeable part of it — where you felt down, sad, or low?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I think there have been periods like that, where I’ll feel low for part of the day, but I don’t know if I’d say it was most days over two years.\n\nIt’s hard to separate normal stress or tiredness from feeling down, if that makes sense. I don’t want to minimize it, but I also don’t want to make it sound more constant than it’s been. I guess I’ve noticed it sometimes, just not in a way I can confidently say was happening most days. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you had times when you felt down or low almost every day, even if it wasn’t all day, for a period of at least two years?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s been a full two years, no.\n\nIf I’m being more precise, it really seems like it started after my divorce was finalized… which was about a year and seven months ago. Since then there have been periods where I’ve felt down very regularly, but I don’t want to say “at least two years” if that isn’t quite accurate.\n\nIs that distinction helpful?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you had days where you felt down or sad nearly every day for periods lasting at least two years?\n\nPatient: Right… I think I might have said this a bit unclearly before. I don’t think it’s been a full two years.\n\nIt’s really been since my divorce was finalized, which was about a year and seven months ago. Since then, yes, I’ve had a lot of days where I’ve felt down or sad, but I don’t want to overstate it and say it lasted at least two years when it hasn’t quite been that long.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you had more days than not where you felt down or sad for most of the day, even if you were still able to get things done?\n\nPatient: I think… not for the full two years, no. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nIt’s been more since the divorce was finalized, so about a year and seven months ago. Since then there have definitely been more stretches where I’ve felt down pretty often, but I’m not sure I should call it the whole past two years. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I know that probably doesn’t sound huge, but it’s been noticeable.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or blaming yourself for things more than usual, or feeling like you're not worth much?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I don’t want to make too much of it, but I do catch myself replaying my marriage and thinking, you know, I must have failed at something pretty basic. Like I should have been better at it somehow.\n\nPart of me knows relationships are more complicated than one person “failing,” but… I still go there in my head. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or sad on most days, even if it's not all day long?\n\nPatient: I’m… still hesitant to say “most days,” honestly. Some days, yes, I do feel kind of low or flat, but then other days I’m more okay, or at least distracted enough that I don’t really notice it as sadness.\n\nSo I don’t know if I should call it most days. I don’t want to overstate it. Maybe it’s more like it’s been there off and on, and I’m not always sure how to measure that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that over the past two weeks, you’ve had more days than not where you felt down or sad, even if it wasn’t all day?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I think… maybe not more days than not, if I’m being careful with the wording. There have definitely been days where I felt down, but there are also days where I’m functioning and I don’t notice it as much.\n\nI don’t want to answer in a way that makes it sound worse than it is. So I’d probably say it’s been intermittent, not every other day necessarily… unless I’m undercounting it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Over the past few weeks, have you had any periods where you felt down or sad for two weeks straight, even if it wasn't every single day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not two weeks straight. I mean, there have been stretches where I’ve felt off and kind of sad, but when you say “two weeks straight,” I’m not sure I can honestly say that.\n\nIt’s more like it comes and goes. I don’t want to minimize it if it matters, but I also don’t want to make it sound constant when I’m not sure it has been.\n\nDoctor: Over the past few months, would you say that on most days you’ve had some period of time—maybe not the whole day, but a significant part of the day—where you felt down, sad, or low?\n\nPatient: Over the past few months… I’m honestly not sure I can say “most days.” There are definitely times during the day where I feel low or sort of heavy, but I don’t know if it’s been a significant part of the day on most days.\n\nI might be kind of splitting hairs, I know, but I don’t want to make it sound more constant than it is. Maybe it’s been there more than I’ve been admitting to myself, but I’m not fully sure how to answer that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you had days where you felt down or sad for most of the day, not just off and on?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s been most of the day, no. There have been days where I feel sad or kind of low, but it tends to come in waves, or I notice it more at certain points.\n\nI’m trying to answer that accurately… I don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely fine, but “most of the day” feels stronger than what I’ve noticed. Does that seem like the right way to describe it?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you take less pleasure or interest in things you used to enjoy, even when you still do them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably true, though I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nI still do some of the things I normally like—like I’ll water the garden, or I’ll go to book club—but a lot of the time it just feels kind of flat. I’ve definitely had stretches where I felt low, but “more days than not” and “most of the day” sounds like more than I can confidently say. I’ve mostly kept functioning, so I may not have been paying close attention to it.\n\nMaybe I’m minimizing it a little, but I don’t think I can honestly say it’s been that persistent. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, would you say that on most days you had some period of time — maybe not the whole day, but a noticeable part of it — where you felt down, sad, or low?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I think there have been periods like that, where I’ll feel low for part of the day, but I don’t know if I’d say it was most days over two years.\n\nIt’s hard to separate normal stress or tiredness from feeling down, if that makes sense. I don’t want to minimize it, but I also don’t want to make it sound more constant than it’s been. I guess I’ve noticed it sometimes, just not in a way I can confidently say was happening most days. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you had times when you felt down or low almost every day, even if it wasn’t all day, for a period of at least two years?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s been a full two years, no.\n\nIf I’m being more precise, it really seems like it started after my divorce was finalized… which was about a year and seven months ago. Since then, yes, I’ve had a lot of days where I’ve felt down or sad, but I don’t want to overstate it and say it lasted at least two years when it hasn’t quite been that long.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you had more days than not where you felt down or sad for most of the day, even if you were still able to get things done?\n\nPatient: I think… not for the full two years, no. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nIt’s been more since the divorce was finalized, so about a year and seven months ago. Since then there have definitely been more stretches where I’ve felt down pretty often, but I’m not sure I should call it the whole past two years. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you had days where you felt down or low more often than not, even if it wasn’t every day or all day long?\n\nPatient: I think the most accurate way to say it is… it’s been going on since my divorce was finalized, which was about a year and seven months ago.\n\nSo, not quite the full two years. But since then, yes, there have been days where I’ve felt low more often than I’d like. Yes… since then, since the divorce was finalized, it’s been going on. That was about a year and seven months ago.
